#5092. Hypercall to launch first Anthropic-linked pre-IPO options market

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Trading is expected to begin Monday, with calls, puts and more complex strategies available in select non-US jurisdictions.

Hypercall is launching what it says is the first options market tied to Anthropic ahead of its IPO.

The onchain options platform expects trading to begin Monday, founder Jake Sylvestre and growth and marketing head Dan Edlebeck told The Block. The contracts will trade around the clock but will not be available to U.S. customers.

The options are synthetic, cash-settled derivatives. This means that traders will not own actual Anthropic shares or receive any voting rights when a contract expires. Payouts will be calculated on a contract's strike price and reference price at expiry.

The launch would add options to a market that already offers Anthropic-linked perpetual futures contracts. Sylvestre said Hypercall supports everything from basic calls and puts, which he called "vanillas," to more advanced spreads and strategies. "It's a fully featured options platform," he said. "Any kind of option you want to structure, we can do."

Hypercall is built on Hyperliquid, where existing Anthropic-linked perps give market makers a way to hedge the risk of selling options before the company's shares begin trading publicly. Edleback said that gives Hypercall a head start: "We're able to provide options on these assets before anyone else can," he said.

The platform already offers options tied to SpaceX and the S&P 500 as well as major cryptos like bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) and ether Ethereum (ETH), according to the executives. Sylvestre previously reported that Hypercall handled $536 million in options notional trading volume for September.

The rollout comes as traders become less confident that Anthropic will even go public this month or even before the end of 2026.

On Polymarket, the chances of a November IPO fell from roughly 88% Tuesday to as low as 51% by Thursday, while the probability of a January 2027 debut jumped from less than 1% to nearly 17%. The market had generated around $566,000 in trading volume.

A similar market on Kalshi, with nearly $4.9 million in volume, put the chances of an IPO before Dec. 1 at 60%, down from around 88%. Contracts on a debut before Jan. 1 and Feb. 1 traded at 63% and 85%, respectively.

Technology shares also sold off sharply on Thursday, with the Nasdaq falling around 6% during the session amid growing concerns around AI infrastructure spending.

Sylvestre said the market backdrop had changed his concerns about pricing the options, from a post-IPO rally to a potential slump.

"People that market make options are really pricing volatility," he said. "How fast it drops matters as much as how much it drops."

An option can expire worthless, but a price swing won't automatically trigger a margin liquidation like with trading perps on leverage.

Anthropic filed its S-1 on Oct. 26, with plans to list on the Nasdaq under the ticker ANT. The Claude developer reported $16.3 billion in first-half revenue and $9 billion in compute spending. Its prospectus revealed that its five largest customers accounted for around 25% of revenue through the end of September.

#5090. E-quipment highlight: the highest-rated mowers on Amazon are ELECTRIC (2026)

Despite decades of market dominance, it seems like the days of loud, finicky, gas-powered mowers may finally be behind us; and I say that because – for the second year in a row! – the highest-rated lawnmowers on Amazon won’t burn a drop of gas.

The latest round of dumb oil wars are ratcheting up fuel prices everywhere while, at the same time, state and local governments continue to tightening restrictions on noise and small-engine emissions. Combined, those things probably mean that your next lawn mower is more likely to plug in than fill up, whether you like it or not. The good news is that. in addition to being quieter, safer, and not poisoning the air you breathe while you walk behind them, electric laenmowers have come a long way in terms of power and performance in just a few years. So much so, in fact, that many of the highest-rated models on Amazon, emphatic period, are electric.

In putting this list together, we looked at the average star ratings of a number of lawn mowers on Amazon, focusing on products that had at least 2,000 reviews and a four-star average or higher, and found that (like last year) battery-electric units from EGO Power, Greenworks, and Worx were among the top-rated mowers, regardless of fuel type.

Here’s the rundown, in alphabetical order (by brand).

Over the past few years, the EGO POWER+ brand has staked a claim to being the gold standard for cordless lawn equipment, thanks to its high-voltage 56V battery platform and premium build quality. The brand’s 21″ POWER+ LM2112 push mower currently carries a 4.5-star average rating from more than 2,200 Amazon reviews and an “Overall Pick” designation.

That’s no surprise, given the LM2112’s 6 ft-lbs. of cutting torque (equivalent to a 150cc ICE), and up to 35 minutes of runtime (call it a suburban yard) from the included 56V 4.0 Ah li-ion battery, three-in-one mulching, bagging and side-discharge capability. It also starts at the push of a button, folds for compact storage and skips the exhaust, maintenance, and oil changes that come along with a comparable gas mower.

With an impressive 4.2-star rating after more than 12,000 verified reviews, the Greenworks 40V 16″ push mower is in rare company among mowers of any kind on Amazon. No surprise, given that the lightweight, cordless mower weighs just 37.5 lbs. and comes with a 4.0Ah battery and charger good for up to 40 minutes of runtime, making it ideal for homeowners with smaller yards who want to ditch gas without wrestling with a heavier machine.

The Greenpower’s 16″ cutting deck is designed for (if not limited to) compact lawns, with a five-position height adjustment and a two-in-one system offering you a choice of mulching (yes) or bagging (no) your lawn clippings.

For bigger, more demanding lawns, Greenworks offers a 4.4-star push mower with a 21″ steel cutting deck that’s powered by an 80V brushless motor and ships with a pair of 2.0 Ah batteries good for up to 40 minutes of runtime with both batteries. Three-in-one functionality for mulching, bagging, or side discharge is included, too, along with push-button starting, seven-position height adjustment, and a rapid charger that can get it back to work in about 30 minutes.

WORX’s 40V 17″ Power Share mower is another strong example of how electric lawn care can be both practical and affordable. The WG743 mates a pair of 20V, 4.0 Ah batteries to deliver 40V of power, and those same batteries work across the brand’s broader Power Share lineup of tools – a lineup that includes string trimmers, power drills, and more.

The WORX mower’s standout feature, though, is its “IntelliCut” technology that works a bit like traction control in your car, automatically increasing torque when the mower hits thicker grass (more traction) and backing off when it doesn’t need the extra power (when it detects wheelspin – or, in this case, cutting head spin). Factor in an intentionally lightweight design, seven-position cutting-height adjustment, and the de rigueur options for mulching or bagging, and you’ve got a versatile little mower for keeping a smaller lawn in shape.

Have a Toro 60v mower since 2019 and nothing but superb. Bought a Toro blower which is as powerful then a gas unit. Cleans snow easily off cars, driveways especially powered. I just ordered the 60v super recycler with delivery on Tuesday. A friend bought one this year and he raved about its dual power button, commercial bag system that throws grass to rear of bagger for complete fill up and full aluminum deck, 3 year warranty. My 20 year old skil 18v tools still perform like new with newer higher powered batteries, along with my 20v updated black and decker tool assortment. Next year giving my like new 15 year old snow blower 2 stage to son buying a Toro 60v 2 stage same as the gas model except battery powered. My gas power washer stays as with a 6 car concrete driveway, 2000 sq ft patio a professional ss surface cleaner requires long running powerful performance.

And check with your state or regional clean air regulator to see if any rebates are available – here’s California’s page, and here’s Colorado’s, but there are constantly shifting incentives available elsewhere too, so even if you’ve looked into those before: look again.

#5088. OpenAI Promises to Foot AI’s Power Bill After Voter Revolt. Who Enforces It?

OpenAI Promises to Foot AI’s Power Bill After Voter Revolt. Who Enforces It?

OpenAI promised Thursday that neighbors of its future AI campuses won't foot the bill for their electricity needs, two days after voters dealt data center expansion a blow at the polls. It also pledged public energy and water figures and limits on secrecy agreements with officials.

Its National Community Compact covers future campuses (a fancy rebranding for the term AI data centers) OpenAI both owns and operates, according to the company’s announcement. A separate policy blueprint asks legislators and regulators to apply similar requirements across the industry.

“We will pay the full cost of powering our compute campuses and ensure local residents do not foot the bill for our energy needs,” OpenAI said in its “national community compact.”

It also promises financial assurances before costly grid upgrades, so other customers aren't left paying if projects shrink or fail. Each AI data center would get a water plan, annual progress reports and independent noise studies.

The national compact does not describe penalties for noncompliance, a dispute process or independent verification of electricity and water figures. Residents could read OpenAI’s disclosures, but the document does not explain how they could compel corrections or recover losses.

The ownership restriction matters, too. Partners including Oracle build and own much of the infrastructure reserved for OpenAI, Reuters reported. The company has not said its national compact automatically covers those partner-owned facilities.

The proposed blueprint goes further on electricity costs. “Policymakers should direct state public utility commissions to create large-load tariffs that require large electricity consumers to pay the incremental costs they create,” it says.

A large-load tariff is a special electricity rate for major users such as data centers. The blueprint also urges mandatory reporting, narrow limits on officials’ NDAs and provisions letting governments reclaim tax breaks when developers miss agreed commitments.

The announcement follows a bruising election for the industry. Voters backed restrictions on data centers in multiple states, while candidates pushing for tighter oversight won key races. The results basically send a message to OpenAI and the broader AI industry that communities want a bigger say in how these facilities are built and how their operations affect the people living nearby.

OpenAI says every future campus it owns and operates will receive its own local compact and an annual public progress report.

TeraWulf's TeraWulf, Inc. (WULF) bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining revenue fell to $13.2 million in the third quarter from $43.4 million a year earlier, a decline of about 69%, while the Nasdaq-listed company reported $63 million in high-performance computing (HPC) lease revenue, up 774% from $7.2 million in the same period last year.

"Lake Mariner is delivering contracted capacity and generating revenue, Justified has the customer, credit support and construction framework to move forward at scale, and Muskie gives us a contracted path to a gigawatt of power," TeraWulf Chairman and CEO Paul Prager said in the earnings release.

Across its portfolio, the company is targeting another 250 MW to 500 MW of contracted critical IT capacity each year. TeraWulf said it had about $2.4 billion in cash, cash equivalents, and restricted cash at the end of the quarter.

TeraWulf shares are up 8.1% at $10.90 in pre-market trading on Friday following the release.

#5086. Hyperliquid Strategies seeks futures registration for perps

The HYPE treasury company spent $785 million on tokens during the quarter and wants to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the US.

A subsidiary of Nasdaq-listed HYPE treasury company Hyperliquid Strategies has applied to become a futures commission merchant (FCM), seeking to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the US.

“Owning HYPE is how we aim to capture economics associated with the Hyperliquid protocol; setting up an FCM is how we intend to help bring markets deployed on Hyperliquid onshore,” said Hyperliquid Strategies CEO David Schamis in the company’s results for the quarter ended Sept. 30, published on Thursday.

Hyperliquid’s HIP-3 framework allows independent operators to launch custom perpetual futures markets on the protocol’s onchain infrastructure by staking 500,000 HYPE tokens ($38.8 million). Proposed HIP-3 features would allow operators to create permissioned markets that restrict participation to approved addresses.

The subsidiary would handle US customer accounts for permissioned markets deployed on Hyperliquid, the company said. Both the FCM application and the proposed markets require regulatory approval.

Intercontinental Exchange, the parent company of the New York Stock Exchange, announced an agreement with Trade[XYZ] on Oct. 29 to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual markets to the US, subject to regulatory approvals. ICE plans to operate a dedicated market and clearing structure.

“Hyperliquid is winning the parts of onchain finance that generate real fees, and nearly all of that value flows back to HYPE holders through programmatic buybacks,” Schamis said.

#5084. Zcash NU7 upgrade goes live, leaves 12,224 ZEC stranded in Sprout pool

Zcash’s NU7 upgrade has rendered about $15 million in legacy Sprout funds unspendable, although a future network upgrade could potentially restore access.

Zcash’s NU7 network upgrade activated Friday, making about $15 millionin ZEC held in the network’s legacy Sprout shielded pool unspendable.

The upgrade took effect at block 3,543,405 at 02:34 UTC, according to blockchain tracker ZecStats, which lists the Sprout pool balance at about 12,224 ZEC.

The upgrade cuts Zcash’s target time between blocks from 75 seconds to 25, although actual block intervals will adjust toward the new target as mining difficulty changes.

Under Zcash Improvement Proposal (ZIP) 2003, NU7 rejects version 4 transactions, the only format that supports spending Sprout funds. The remaining coins cannot be transferred under the new rules, but have not been burned.

The specification leaves open the possibility that a future network upgrade could restore access, either by re-enabling version 4 transactions or introducing another mechanism to retrieve the funds. However, it warns that “the ability to spend Sprout funds may never be re-enabled.”

NU7 also introduces a Network Sustainability Mechanism that removes 60% of transaction fees from circulation for eventual reissuance through future mining rewards, starting around February 2031.

The upgrade preserves Zcash’s 21 million ZEC supply cap and halving schedule, while introducing limits on shielded activity designed to reduce wallet synchronization demands.

#5083. Battery electric, solar-powered reefer units reach their first customers

The first regular production solar-powered electric trailer refrigeration unit (TRU) in Australia has hit the road with a real customer. Family-owned transport giant Wickham Freight Lines took delivery of the revolutionary reefer just this week.

Refrigerated trailers (reefers) are critical for hauling fresh food and flowers from the farm to your local market — the problem is that they pollute like crazy, powered by high sulfur, red-dye diesel-powered generators that run around the clock, even if the truck pulling them is fully electric. That constant diesel burn spews CO₂ and NOx into industrial and residential areas alike, and contributes significantly to fleets’ carbon emissions and noise pollution at a time when a number of municipalities are moving to regulate both.

It is, as they say, “a real problem,” but Sunswap Endurance cracked it with a solar-powered reefer that can keep food and pharmaceuticals cold in transit without burning a drop of diesel fuel – and now, they have real customers.

One of Australia’s largest privately owned trucking companies, Wickham Freight Lines decided to purchase the Sunswap Endurance solar trailer units following an initial trial, running from Sydney to Brisbane and back this past summer on a route covering a total of 1,670 km (~1,040 miles) over more than 30 hours of driving.

After Wickham’s drivers finished the round trip with over 65% of the battery’s capacity still left, the purchase was a no-brainer.

“I put the Sunswap Endurance into the fleet and told the team to stretch it — treat it as a normal unit,” explains Graham Keogh, director at Wickham Freight Lines. “We were genuinely impressed. I’m a true believer. I love the technology and I’m not a true tech person whatsoever. You can just see that it’s a winner … the performance, reliability and operational savings were beyond what we expected. For a business like ours, running a large fleet across the east coast, with a heavy focus on refrigerated and cold chain freight, this technology makes real commercial sense.”

Sunswap Endurance first showed their new solar reefer unit at HVIA TruckShowX in Hunter Valley this May past. It’s compelling, insultingly simple technology and I, for one, hope this customer delivery is just the first of many. Let us know what you think of these guys, in the comments.

Solar powered refrigeration truck looks like a natural combination. Cost of the solar is offset somewhat by not needing diesel tank, motor, and generator. Majority of savings probably come form not needing constant refueling (fuel + labor), or in not needing to overhaul the engine periodically.

I would be very interested in knowing how quickly the system pays for itself.

#5081. Bitcoin Fixes This? Paraguay President Resigns After Bank Lockout Hits 60% of Country

Bitcoin Fixes This? Paraguay President Resigns After Bank Lockout Hits 60% of Country

Paraguayan President Santiago Peña announced his resignation Thursday after the collapse of digital lender Ueno Bank left roughly three million customers unable to access their accounts, turning a banking failure into a political emergency.

The announcement did not end the confrontation. Paraguay’s lower house voted 78-0 to impeach Peña minutes later, sending the charges to the Senate as Vice President Pedro Alliana prepared to take over, per AP.

The bank’s shutdown has also revived an argument familiar to Bitcoin holders: Money held by a financial intermediary is only as accessible as that institution allows. Self-custodied Bitcoin works differently, although it comes with risks of its own.

As the Bitcoin OGs would say, “Bitcoin fixes this.”

Paraguay’s central bank placed Ueno into resolution after regulators found its solvency ratio had fallen below half the legal minimum. The process, announced on November 3, followed accounting adjustments to the bank’s financial statements and left its app, cards, and transfers out of service.

Ueno claimed around 2.9 million customers, more than 60% of Paraguay’s adults. It also held roughly $800 million in public-sector deposits, including pension and health funds, according to AP, complicating an already painful freeze on ordinary savings and wages.

Peña previously held a stake in a company linked to Ueno’s owners, a relationship that fueled allegations of conflicts of interest. He has denied wrongdoing and said he sold his interest in March 2025, according to Reuters.

“My greatest wish is that the return of those funds be guaranteed, that the Deposit Guarantee Fund fulfill its purpose,” Peña said in his resignation address. The Deposit Guarantee Fund would be the equivalent of the FDIC in the United States.

Mensaje del Presidente de la República, Santiago Peña, al pueblo paraguayo. pic.twitter.com/RBz6IfFV9L

— Santiago Peña (@SantiPenap) November 5, 2026

Outside Congress, the anger was less diplomatic. “Santiago Peña and the Colorado Party have stolen all our savings,” protester Luis Pineda told AP. “We are fed up with 70 years of plunder.”

The central bank says Ueno’s failure is an isolated case, not evidence of a broader banking collapse. It has announced a deposit guarantee of up to 228.3 million guaraníes per depositor, roughly $36,000, with initial payments targeting eligible accounts holding no more than 10 million guaraníes.

Bitcoin was born during another banking crisis. Its first block, mined in January 2009, included a newspaper headline about a second bailout for British banks, a historical detail long cited by supporters who distrust reliance on financial intermediaries.

A Bitcoin holder who controls a wallet’s private keys can authorize transfers from the wallet without asking a third party, like a bank, to unlock an account. That is the principle behind the crypto saying “not your keys, not your coins,” and the distinction from leaving Bitcoin on an exchange that can itself freeze withdrawals.

But Bitcoin is not perfect. Self-custody has its own risks: Forget your keys or lose them and there is no way you can recover your funds, there is nobody who can assist you, and no law enforcement agency who can do anything for you.

Paraguay’s central bank said Thursday its first guarantee payments would go to depositors with up to 10 million guaraníes, no Ueno debts and an account at another financial institution.

#5080. Binance’s $6.3B hack leaves questions over hot wallet safeguards

Experts say blockchain records show repeated wallet drains but cannot establish Binance’s internal signing controls or transaction limits.

Binance’s $6.3 billion security incident has left unanswered questions about how the exchange managed its hot wallets and limited the amount attackers could access.

Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said on Oct. 30 that the attack affected the exchange’s own hot wallet. Most customer assets were held in offline cold storage and moved to hot wallets only when users requested withdrawals.

Wallet balances offer only a partial picture of an exchange’s exposure to an attack. Public blockchain records can show funds moving but cannot establish the internal controls governing those transfers, CertiK analyst Jonathan Riss told Cointelegraph.

In its initial report Tuesday, Binance said attackers accessed an internal wallet-signing service to move funds outside its normal withdrawal process. Binance said some technical details would remain confidential to prevent further attacks.

The exchange did not respond to Cointelegraph’s questions about its wallet balances and safeguards.

Rivals including Coinbase, Gate and Upbit have disclosed their cold-storage ratios following the attack.

Riss said the $6.3 billion figure represented the cumulative value of assets affected across 10 blockchains. On Ethereum, attackers repeatedly drained a hot wallet as new deposits arrived.

When attackers can obtain valid signatures, their potential access depends on what the signing system allows them to transfer over time, he said. A wallet’s balance at a single moment does not capture that exposure.

Related: EIP-7702 didn’t cause Binance hack, but may have amplified losses

Transaction limits enforced by the application requesting a signature can be bypassed if that application is compromised, Riss said. Limits enforced independently by the signing system may still restrict transfers.

“A comparison with other exchanges isn’t really possible from the outside,” Riss added. “Public information on internal signing architecture is limited everywhere, and reserve distribution and proof-of-reserves attestations show what sits where, not how a withdrawal is authorized.”

BitGo chief product officer Eugene Hahr also declined to speculate about Binance’s internal safeguards.

“The breakdown of custody arrangements is a question Binance is best placed to answer,” he said.

Idan Ofrat, co-founder and chief product officer at Fireblocks, said exchanges typically keep enough funds in hot wallets to cover a few days of expected withdrawals, often representing a low single-digit percentage of assets under management.

Exchanges generally hold the bulk of customer assets in cold storage, while some use intermediate wallets that require manual approval for transfers to hot wallets, he said.

Related: Binance traces $6.3B hack to fake job interviews, compromised AI tool

Alex Tobol, chief technology officer at Telegram-based crypto wallet Walt, said holding billions of dollars in hot wallets is not inherently unusual for an exchange of Binance’s size.

Hot wallets generally process withdrawals automatically, Tobol said, but replenishing them from cold storage should require a separate treasury decision and manual approval.

Hahr said controls applied before signing, including transaction limits, approved withdrawal addresses and additional authorization requirements, can help contain losses when a system is compromised.

Magazine: Binance hackers raced to move billions before it could be frozen

#5076. XCMG debuts new, fully autonomous electric terminal tractor [video]

The new XCMG SDA411 concept terminal truck is fully electric, has no cabin, and an L4 autonomous tech stack that’s purpose-built for unmanned logistics – and, if XCMG get their way, it looks like the future of drayage truck operations.

Whether you call them terminal trucks, yard dogs, or anything else, these heavy-duty trucks do work that’s ideally suited to electrification. It’s all low-speed, high-torque, short distance, precision operation, all the time. And the quiet, vibration-free, instant power operation of electric drive is in its element.

Chinese equipment brand XCMG gets it, and they’re taking the electric terminal truck concept a step further with the debut of an all-new, cab-less design that’s 100% autonomous.

“With no cab and an L4 autonomous driving system, the platform is purpose-built for unmanned logistics,” said XCMG, on LinkedIn. “Its 360° perception system enables autonomous navigation, obstacle avoidance, task execution and parking, supporting continuous mixed-traffic operations in demanding environments.”

That same post included a video showing the SDA411 make its way around XCMG’s campus, making some emergency stops, and pulling up to a charger for a human attendant to plug it in (I guess they haven’t heard about Fortescue’s trick with the KUKA welding robot, yet). You can check out the post for yourself, below.

As for the autonomous aspect, while I generally side with labor and humanity over management and technology, there’s some truth to the industry claims that young people don’t want to drive trucks anymore, even with the promise of six-figure salaries.

Combine that with an aging workforce and not enough new talent entering the field, and virtually every industrial sector is struggling with a serious operator shortage. Autonomous equipment like the SDA411 could help address that shortage, keeping goods moving even when there aren’t enough drivers to go around. And rather than simply replacing workers, the technology could let experienced operators oversee multiple vehicles, take on supervisory roles and stay in the industry longer, while making these jobs accessible to people who might not otherwise be able to do them.

It doesn’t have to be autonomous because it’s electric it only needs to be powered by electric motors not gas to be better in every way.

If XCMG get their way, we’ll get to see how that all plays out somewhat sooner than later. In the meantime, we’ll have to settle for hearing what you think about the SDA411 and how it could impact brands like Kalmar and Orange EV in the comments.

#5075. GOPAX halts crypto trading, withdrawals to move all assets to cold storage

South Korea’s GOPAX suspended trading, deposits and standard withdrawals after a hacking threat, with manual withdrawals available through support.

South Korean crypto exchange GOPAX suspended trading, deposits and standard withdrawals Friday after receiving an email threatening a hack, according to local newspaper Kyunghyang Shinmun.

A GOPAX representative told the newspaper that no hack or attempted theft had occurred at that point.

GOPAX said it halted deposits and standard withdrawals at 5:55 am UTC. Trading stopped at 6:53 UTC, according to a second announcement. All unfilled orders were canceled.

GOPAX said it plans to transfer all crypto assets to offline wallets to protect users following a series of leaks and hacking incidents affecting financial institutions. Its notices cited financial regulators’ recommendations.

Cointelegraph reached out to GOPAX for comment but had not received a response by publication time.

Customers can still request withdrawals through GOPAX’s support center, but the exchange warned that manual verification and approvals could delay processing.

The suspension came days after South Korea’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) urged domestic exchanges to increase cold-wallet holdings following the $6.3 billion Binance hack. South Korean exchanges must keep at least 80% of customer crypto assets in cold storage.

Financial Supervisory Service data previously showed that GOPAX held 80.54% of user assets offline at the end of September, near the required minimum.

In October, the FSC reported cybersecurity deficiencies at 130 of 555 financial companies reviewed nationwide. The regulator also identified GOPAX operator Streami and crypto exchange Prabang among 16 companies involved in earlier security incidents, prompting plans for tighter cybersecurity oversight.

South Korean exchange Prabang also suspended services after detecting abnormal withdrawals from its hot wallet on Oct. 24. The exchange reported about 100 million won ($70,000) in losses, according to Yonhap News Agency. Prabang said it would reimburse affected customers using company funds.

#5074. Claude Gets Personal Finance—Anthony Pompliano Accuses Anthropic of Copying His Startup

Claude Gets Personal Finance—Anthony Pompliano Accuses Anthropic of Copying His Startup

Anthropic launched a personal finance feature for Claude on Thursday, letting users connect bank accounts and ask the chatbot what they have spent, what bills are due, and how much money they have left.

The announcement came with an accusation from crypto investor Anthony Pompliano, who says Anthropic tried to get inside information from his competing startup, Silvia, months before the rollout.

Claude can then summarize spending, spot duplicate charges or unexpected fees, explain interest payments, and build charts or bill calendars. Anthropic's example shows a user asking where their money went this month, then following up to find out what remains before payday.

"Access is read-only, and Claude can't move, manage, or invest your money," Anthropic wrote on X. "Like the rest of Claude, Personal Finance has no ads or sponsored recommendations," the company added.

The assistant can see balances, credit limits, account names and types, the last four digits of account numbers, and up to 24 months of transactions from accounts users approve. According to Anthropic's documentation, conversations inside the Personal Finance hub are not used to train its models, but account balances may lag and AI-generated figures should be checked against bank records.

Also, how Anthropic tracks its users’ financial data and uses it beyond model training is not completely clear—and this is extremely valuable information for big data companies.

Pompliano, whose Silvia product bills itself as a personal AI chief financial officer, posted a lengthy complaint on X shortly after Claude's announcement. He alleged that Anthropic personnel began approaching Silvia engineers in April, supposedly because they were interested in the startup's growth and computing needs.

"Anthropic was fishing for details on how we built our product and why it was so popular," he wrote. Pompliano also described an alleged steak dinner involving Anthropic representatives, claiming someone told the Silvia team that the meal was on CEO Dario Amodei's tab.

His engineers declined to divulge sensitive information, he claimed, adding that someone at Anthropic warned his team about the upcoming launch days earlier.

"Today, Anthropic launched a Claude wrapper that is masquerading as a personal finance product," Pompliano wrote. He also claimed Silvia's computing costs are roughly 97% lower when using its own models and hardware instead of frontier AI models, which most likely mean they are using a local model, likely finetuned for the startup’s specific purpose.

That said, Anthropic was not the first general-purpose chatbot maker to move into personal finance, either. OpenAI launched similar bank connections for ChatGPT in May, also using Plaid for read-only access to financial information, and Agents like Hermes, Open Claw, or Grok Bot can also do pretty similar tasks given the proper skills, permissions, and instructions.

Anthropic says Personal Finance is a first step and is exploring partnerships with licensed professionals and regulated financial firms. For now, the beta is restricted to U.S. customers on three paid Claude plans.

#5072. Greece approves 10% tax on crypto gains starting in 2027

Greece has approved a 10% tax on cryptocurrency gains starting in 2027, with an exemption for investors whose annual net gains do not exceed 500 euros.

Greece’s parliament approved legislation imposing a 10% tax on cryptocurrency gains, establishing new rules for investors as the country prepares to introduce the levy in 2027.

The Hellenic Parliament passed the tax measures Thursday as part of a broader bill covering loan oversight, debt protections and financial market regulation.

The bill introduces a 10% tax on profits from crypto asset sales but exempts investors whose annual gains do not exceed 500 euros ($560), local financial publication Oikonomikos Tachydromos reported Friday. The new framework is scheduled to take effect on Jan. 1, 2027.

The legislation also imposes a 15% tax on income from staking, crypto lending and providing liquidity to decentralized protocols. Using crypto to buy goods or services may also trigger capital gains tax, while swapping one crypto asset for another does not generate taxable gains, the report notes.

Greece’s Ministry of National Economy and Finance first proposed the tax in October. The legislation gives investors 12 months to voluntarily declare previously unreported crypto gains. They must pay the resulting tax within 60 days of filing to avoid penalties and interest.

#5071. Amflow launches two powerful new electric bikes, including its first SUV-style e-bike

Amflow, the electric bicycle brand that burst onto the scene with some of the most powerful and lightweight electric mountain bikes on the market, is expanding its lineup with two major releases. The company has announced global availability of its new Amflow TL Carbon, while also bringing its removable-battery Amflow PR Carbon series to the US for the first time.

Both models are powered by the latest Avinox mid-drive motor systems, which have quickly earned a reputation for delivering impressive power in surprisingly compact packages.

The biggest departure from Amflow’s roots is the new TL Carbon, which takes the company’s high-performance mountain biking DNA and applies it to a more versatile SUV-style electric bike. While less common in the US (ironically), SUV-style e-bikes are essentially a hybrid electric bicycle designed to act as an all-in-one, “do-it-all” vehicle combining the rugged build and off-road capability of an electric mountain bike with the practical, everyday utility of a city or trekking bike.

Rather than focusing exclusively on technical trails and aggressive mountain biking, the TL Carbon is designed to handle everything from daily commuting to touring and off-road adventures. And with Avinox’s RS800 battery featuring 800Wh of capacity, it’s got plenty of energy on board for diverse adventures.

The full-suspension bike uses the Avinox M2 motor, delivering up to 125 Nm of torque, which should provide more than enough grunt for steep climbs, heavy loads, and just about anything else riders can throw at it.

Avinox e-bike drive systems are known for many things, but chief among them is the extremely high power and torque they offer, eclipsing most other mid-drive motor systems on the market.

Despite that power and versatility, the TL Carbon weighs as little as 22.6 kg (49.8 lb). It also boasts an impressive maximum total weight capacity of 200 kg (441 lb), including the bike itself, rider, and cargo carried on its front and rear racks.

That’s a compelling combination for riders who want the performance of a premium electric mountain bike but need something practical enough to replace a car for everyday trips.

And perhaps most surprisingly, the price isn’t completely outrageous. The Amflow TL Carbon starts at $3,899 in the US and €3,499 in Germany, positioning it competitively against premium European trekking and SUV-style e-bikes.

Meanwhile, American riders are finally getting access to the Amflow PR Carbon series, which had previously launched in other markets.

Unlike Amflow’s earlier electric mountain bikes, the PR series features a removable battery, addressing one of the biggest practical complaints about high-performance eMTBs with integrated batteries.

The new 800 Wh battery weighs just 4 kg (8.8 lb) and can be removed for convenient indoor charging or swapped for a fresh battery to extend a ride. That’s especially useful for riders who don’t have a convenient charging outlet near their bike storage area.

Amflow claims up to 158 km (98 miles) of range under ideal conditions, though that’s based on relatively gentle riding on flat pavement in Eco mode. Riders tackling technical trails with high assistance levels should expect considerably less. But any way you slice it, 800 Wh is a huge battery for eMTBs and should deliver plenty of range under most conditions.

The standard PR Carbon uses the Avinox M2 motor with up to 125 Nm of torque, while the higher-end PR Carbon Pro upgrades to the more powerful Avinox M2S motor, producing up to 150 Nm of torque.

In the US, the PR Carbon starts at $4,999, while the PR Carbon Pro commands $6,799. European pricing starts at €4,499 and €5,899, respectively, based on German pricing.

All three bikes are available beginning today through ⁠Amflow’s official website and its network of authorized dealers. In the US, both models generally arrive as Class 1 e-bikes (no throttle) with a top speed of 20 mph (32 km/h), though Amflow models can typically be unlocked to Class 3 performance with a top speed of 28 mph (45 km/h).

Amflow has been one of the more interesting brands to watch in the premium e-bike space, largely because of how aggressively it has pushed the boundaries of motor performance and bike weight.

But these latest releases show that the company is thinking beyond simply building increasingly powerful electric mountain bikes.

The TL Carbon is particularly interesting to me because it brings that impressive Avinox performance to a more practical platform. As someone who spends a minority of my riding time in the dirt, Amflow suddenly looks a lot more interesting to me, personally. Not everyone needs a dedicated eMTB, but plenty of riders would appreciate a lightweight, powerful, well-equipped electric bike that can handle both weekday commuting and weekend adventures.

And the PR Carbon’s removable battery is a welcome development, especially as the industry continues to recognize that convenience and serviceability matter just as much as outright performance.

With the Avinox ecosystem continuing to expand across more bike brands and categories, Amflow is also facing increasing competition from other manufacturers using the same impressive motors. That makes it all the more important for the brand to differentiate itself through thoughtful bike design, not just headline-grabbing power figures.

#5069. Zcash's NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Recycling 60% of Fees to Fund Future Block Rewards

Zcash's NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Recycling 60% of Fees to Fund Future Block Rewards

"NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here," the project's official account tweeted.

NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here. $ZEC pic.twitter.com/rPaSFe9h7j

"This is a huge milestone for Shielded Labs," Jason McGee of Shielded Labs, the Zcash development organization behind the mechanism, tweeted ahead of the activation.

The NSM tackles a problem Zcash inherited from Bitcoin: whether, as block subsidies decline with each halving, transaction fees alone will be enough to pay miners to secure the network. Under the mechanism, 60% of transaction fees will be removed from circulation and gradually reissued through future block rewards, while preserving the 21 million ZEC supply cap. Reissuance will begin in 2031, with the recycled funds spread along a smooth curve on top of the existing halving schedule.

The NSM was Shielded Labs' first project and builds on an idea that developer Nate Wilcox proposed years ago, originally called the Zcash Posterity Fund, McGee said. A separate proposal under consideration would require Zcash Community Grants to contribute 50,000 ZEC from its treasury to the NSM.

— Jason McGee 🛡 (@aquietinvestor) November 5, 2026

NU7 also limits how much private transaction activity can fit into each block, reducing the data wallets must scan to find their payments, according to the upgrade specification.

Shielded Labs' original design would have replaced Zcash's halvings with a smooth issuance curve. Community panels supported that change, but coinholders did not, so an alternative design that kept the halvings was developed instead.

"We still think the original design was simpler and better, but we respect the process that led us here," McGee wrote. "It should be difficult to change the protocol, and the process should resist capture by any single organization, group, or influential individual."

The upgrade arrives as Zcash's community debates the future of Zcash Community Grants (ZCG) and its share of the Dev Fund, the slice of block rewards that pays for development.

The five organizations that coordinate Zcash's coinholder voting, Project Tachyon, Shielded Labs, Valar Group, the Zcash Foundation and ZODL, have set out an expedited timeline for proposals and polling, saying "tensions were high" and that a drawn-out debate risked "deepening divisions within the Zcash community."

Proposals are due by November 8, with polls for coinholders and community panels running from November 16 to November 30. If rough consensus emerges, the groups will support a further network upgrade, NU 7.1, to implement it.

#5067. All aboard! Massachusetts is getting 10 new electric transit trains

The Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority (MBTA) board of directors voted unanimously Thursday to approve the purchase of a new generation of battery-powered public transit trains – and they can’t arrive soon enough.

“(MBTA) has, obviously, an immediate need for locomotives,” explains William Wolfgang, the MBTA’s senior director of vehicle engineering. The new machines will replace the oldest locomotives in the MBTA fleet, GP40s built more than 50 years ago and F40s that are well over 30 years old. In a presentation to the MTBA board, MTBA said 13 of the GP40s have, “exceeded their useful life,” while the F40s will reach the end of their life between 2029 and 2035.

The MBTA will put in an order for 20 Siemens-built “Charger” locomotives; 10 Tier 4 diesel-powered hybrid electric units and 10 dual-mode battery/catenary electric units – and, beyond trackside power for charging the batteries, there are other challenges presented by going electric, most notably using existing cab cars to control the battery-electric units. “We’re going to have to work with railroad operations, engineering, and the Brotherhood of [Locomotive Engineers and Trainmen] to try and determine the best way to handle that,” adds Wolfgang. “That will be an upcoming change order once we get into the design review process.”

The total bill comes out to $310.4 million. A combination of state and federal funding will pay for the order, including a recent $200 million transfer from the Massachusetts Department of Transportation.

The contract includes an option for additional locomotives, although details were not discussed during the meeting. Delivery of the new Siemens Charger trains is set to begin in 2029, with all 20 set to be in service by the end of 2030. Siemens has delivered 440 Charger model hybrid- and battery-electric locomotives globally at the time of this writing.

Bitcoin consolidated as fears over US AI regulation spread to Asia, causing South Korea to announce measures to calm stock-market volatility.

Bitcoin (BTC) held above $75,000 on Friday as a sell-off in US technology stocks spread to Asian markets.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD trading near its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), at $75,733.

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50-week SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

The pair fell as much as 6.6% on Thursday as US stock markets saw sustained selling throughout the Wall Street trading session. The S&P 500 and tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite closed down 4.4% and 5.5%, respectively.

Nasdaq Composite Index one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

On Friday, Asian stocks extended the sell-off. South Korea’s KOSPI Composite Index fell more than 8% shortly after the open, triggering a circuit breaker that halted trading for 20 minutes. The index then pared losses to close 6% lower at 5,059.69 points, its lowest close since March 31.

KOSPI one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

“We recognize with gravity the expanding volatility in global stock markets following the U.S. midterm elections,” Chosun quoted an unnamed government source as saying.

El-Erian said the backlash reflected “genuine concerns,” information failures and behavioral factors.

#5064. Former FBI Agent Pleads Guilty to Stealing $2.5M in Crypto From 35 Investigations

Former FBI Agent Pleads Guilty to Stealing $2.5M in Crypto From 35 Investigations

A former FBI agent has pleaded guilty to stealing about $2.47 million in cryptocurrency using wallet credentials the bureau had collected during its own investigations, the Justice Department said on Thursday.

Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, of Ashburn, Virginia, pleaded guilty to wire fraud and money laundering in federal court in Alexandria. He faces up to 20 years in prison and is scheduled to be sentenced on March 4, 2027.

Former FBI agent Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, of Ashburn, pled guilty today to wire fraud and money laundering related to using his access to steal cryptocurrency.@FBIWFO https://t.co/jDVtSiAM3H

— U.S. Attorney EDVA (@EDVAnews) November 5, 2026

Yaroch worked at the FBI's Boston Division from 2017 to early 2025 on a squad investigating an unnamed "adversarial nation state," before moving to the bureau's Counterintelligence and Espionage Division, where he had access to FBI systems holding crypto credentials gathered in investigations, according to prosecutors.

Beginning no later than December 2024, he used that access to steal crypto from wallets controlled by subjects of other investigations, by nation-state actors, and from at least one wallet the FBI had created for an operation. He made about 100 fraudulent transfers using credentials from 35 separate FBI investigations, then swapped the stolen funds into other cryptocurrencies and moved them through further transactions to hide their origin. He used some of the money to pay off credit cards, car payments and other loans.

Yaroch also planned a new life abroad. He asked ChatGPT how to maximize profits from investments and where to live in the European Union, and after the chatbot suggested buying a vineyard in Portugal, he gave Portuguese lawyers power of attorney to obtain a tax identification number for him, prosecutors said. He arranged to travel to Portugal in September.

His actions compromised multiple FBI investigations into criminal groups and nation-state adversaries, as well as sources and methods used in foreign intelligence collection, according to the plea agreement's statement of facts.

Yaroch turned himself in. On July 29, he broke down while telling a former colleague at the Justice Department's National Security Division that he had made "very poor decisions" involving crypto wallets, according to an FBI affidavit filed in the case. He then self-reported to FBI headquarters, saying he had become frustrated that the bureau "could not or would not act" against an adversary's crypto accounts and had decided to "take matters into his own hands."

At the time, Yaroch said he had made about 10 to 12 transfers worth under $1 million, far less than the figures in his plea. Agents searching his home two days later seized a Trezor hardware wallet, three passports and a slip of paper bearing seed phrases, according to the affidavit. His Kraken account held about $188,600, mostly in U.S. dollars and the USDC stablecoin, with smaller amounts in Squid (QUID) and Bitcoin. A further $933,800 sat in Suilend, a lending protocol on the Sui blockchain, which he had accessed through the Slush wallet app. Yaroch told agents he chose Suilend "simply because he liked that the logo was a water droplet."

Agents transferred about $925,000 from those accounts to government-controlled wallets, the affidavit said. The roughly $165,600 held as U.S. dollars on Kraken could not be moved the same way.

Decrypt reported on the charges against Yaroch in August.

#5063. Binance hackers had 8 weeks of access before $6.3B exploit, Security Alliance estimates

The cybersecurity company flagged similar multi-stage intrusion patterns behind other high-profile crypto heists, including the $388 million Bitget hack.

Non-profit cybersecurity organization Security Alliance (SEAL) estimates that the North Korea-linked TraderTraitor gained access to Binance’s systems about eight weeks before the $6.3 billion exploit on Oct. 29.

The hacking group gained access to Binance’s systems sometime in September, wrote SEAL in a Thursday report, adding that threat actors can navigate these systems with a “high degree of stealth over prolonged periods of time” after initial intrusion.

SEAL identified at least four “front companies” associated with TraderTraitor’s fake recruitment campaigns in 2026, including citalab.net, citalab.com, bitfit.io and oddium.io.

The cybersecurity organization’s analysis pointed to similar multi-stage intrusion patterns in other high-profile crypto hacks. It estimated that attackers had about six weeks of access before the $388 million Bitget hack, six weeks of access to LayerZero before the vulnerability that led to the $293 million Kelp DAO exploit and about three weeks before conducting the $24 million AFX exploit.

Cointelegraph contacted Binance for comment on the estimates but did not receive a response by publication. Binance attributed the $6.3 billion hack to a fake job interview that enabled hackers to compromise an employee device and steal credentials to breach the exchange’s wallet infrastructure.

Binance traced the attack campaign back to June, when a separate employee’s device was compromised. The exchange detected the incident and replaced the device, but said that attackers had already gathered information about its team and wallet infrastructure, according to a Tuesday X post.

The US Federal Bureau of Investigation attributed the attack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor, in a Saturday report. Kim Yo Jong, sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, dismissed the accusations.

#5062. Range Energy shows how its eTrailer saves almost as much as a Tesla Semi

Range Energy, a company working to electrify trucking through the use of a trailer with its own battery and motors, has published an analysis showing how much fleets can save through electrification – and just at the right time, with diesel at record highs.

You may have heard a little something about diesel prices lately, which have just about doubled in less than a year due to Donald Trump’s war of choice in Iran.

Diesel is now at record highs, and it’s devastating fleets and truck drivers and anyone who uses any products that are delivered on trucks (so, everyone). It’s an urgent problem that needs an urgent solution, and solutions are not forthcoming from the responsible entities.

So, fleets have to seek another solution, and Range Energy would like to be the one that provides it.

Range is a startup based in California, founded and staffed by former Tesla employees and others from around the EV industry. It’s building an electrified trailer which we demoed at ACT Expo in 2024 (really, watch it, it’s a very cool demo) which promises to cut fleet fuel use significantly.

It does this by having its own battery and motors inside the trailer, meaning the tractor doesn’t have to work nearly as hard as the trailer can effectively pull its own weight. Even though the tractor still runs on diesel, it uses a whole lot less of it.

This differs from the other popular option for fleets: full electrification. There are a number of electric semi tractors available, and fleets have been moving to install them, getting their depots ready for charging. But Range lets you bring your own tractor, and electrifies the trailer instead.

Range previously published some early findings, showing a 36% reduction in fuel use in a testing scenario. It also did a pilot program with an egg farm in California, which improved the fuel efficiency of the farm’s diesel fleet by 50-70%.

Now Range has published its own white paper showing comparative savings that fleets can achieve with either its own solution, the eTrailer, or Tesla’s solution, the fully electric Tesla Semi. The paper does not cover the various other electric semi tractors available on the market today, just a face-off between Range and Tesla.

The analysis goes through several scenarios, mostly focusing on refrigerated trailer units (“reefers”), which is the first product Range is focusing on.

Trailer electrification is a great idea for reefers, because they usually fill up on volume rather than weight (called “cubing out”), meaning battery weight doesn’t eat into maximum trailer payload. Batteries also offer power to run refrigeration, meaning you don’t need to have a diesel generator on the trailer.

The upshot of the whole thing is that: fleets can save money with the Range eTrailer, and can theoretically save more money with the Tesla Semi, but the numbers are relatively close in the most common scenario, and the Tesla scenarios have a much wider spread depending on what implementation you consider.

For example, if you only use public chargers rather than your own depot, the Tesla Semi will be more costly because energy will be more expensive to your fleet than if you bought it at commercial utility rates. So, even though installing chargers in your depot sounds expensive, it will pencil out better in the end.

The best money savings came from having two Semis share the same charger, but that requires tight timings and overnight driving.

Another important feature Range points out is build time for a project. The nice thing about the eTrailer is it can slot into fleets immediately with little operational change required, whereas the larger charging needs of an electric tractor) tend to require bigger projects to get a depot ready with chargers (but despite the higher upfront costs, you get better long-term savings, since you avoid diesel entirely with an electric tractor).

In the end, Range found that its solution saves a little more money than the Tesla Semi does in most scenarios, but the difference is quite narrow, with each option having its strengths and weaknesses. Both solutions are better than diesel in most scenarios, but not all of them.

You don’t necessarily need to choose between the two, as an electrified trailer can still be used with an electric tractor. It works the same way in either case – using its own motors to take some of the effort off the tractor. This means extended range, for longer hauls with light loads that couldn’t quite make it on the Tesla Semi’s inherent range. And the benefit of being able to run the reefer while disconnected from the tractor or shore power remains as well.

The analysis has plenty of limitations. It has to estimate future diesel costs (it used a number below current prices, but still historically high), didn’t cover financing costs, it doesn’t have real-world data for the Tesla Semi, didn’t cover electrified tractor or trailer options from other companies, can’t analyze individual charging costs site-to-site since each is specific to a utility company, didn’t cover the myriad incentives available in various regions, and most of all it didn’t consider diesel emissions, which are a huge societal cost to everyone that electrification reduces (and full electrification reduces more).

So in the end, every fleet has a different use case, and should run the numbers themselves to see how either solution would fit in. But in the generalized case, it looks like there are plenty of savings available out there, whether through tractor or trailer electrification.

A good idea in theory but there are three trailers for every tractor-cab because trailers spend a lot of time sitting idle. For specific closed-loop customers and routes this can very very helpful but not as a general solution.

You know what can offer relief right now? Importing Class 8 Trucks. China is selling 280,000 heavy duty EV trucks this year. Trade barriers hurt economies.

Unfortunately, given diesel prices are at record highs right now, both of the options in the paper are a little suboptimal. Tesla Semis have been on order for almost ten years now, and Tesla only just claimed it has started production in earnest. And Range is still about a year out from volume production of its eTrailer, which it says will start in late 2027 – so, while an eTrailer will be able to slot into a fleet immediately when it’s available, it’s not available yet, which puts a damper on that potential benefit.

So neither can offer immediate relief, but both offer good options for fleets to plan for the next inevitable oil shock – which is why Range didn’t base its analysis solely on current prices (remember, we just had another shock in 2022, this nonsense happens all the time, maybe it’s time to stop using oil). But, the numbers in the paper for the Tesla Semi can be compared to numbers for other electric tractors already available today, which can in turn be compared to the costs of the eTrailer, for fleets so inclined to make that comparison.

#5060. Morning Minute: Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Crater

Morning Minute: Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Crater

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

📉 Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Crater

On Wednesday, the market decided a Democratic Congress was good news. Stocks had their best day in three months, Bitcoin climbed back over $80,800, and Trump posted on Truth Social that the market “just hit another Record High” with 401(k)s “through the roof.”

On Thursday, the market changed its mind.

The S&P 500 fell 4.36%, its worst session since the Liberation Day tariffs in April 2025. The Nasdaq dropped 5.52% and closed in correction, more than 10% below its record from last month. The Dow shed 1,833 points.

The damage concentrated in one place. The iShares Semiconductor ETF fell more than 12%, its worst day since March 2020. Micron lost 17.5%, AMD 17.3%, Marvell 16.4%, Nvidia 9.3%. The Magnificent Seven shed roughly $1 trillion in market value. The VIX touched 28.09 intraday, its highest since March. By the close, the U.S. stock market had lost $3T in value - more than the entire market cap of crypto.

Notably, the thing that spooked investors was the same election result that lifted everything a day earlier. Several states where Democrats won have active local opposition to data center construction, and the market spent Thursday pricing in slower permitting and tighter oversight of the AI buildout. Ipek Ozkardeskaya at Swissquote put it simply: “We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout.” Rate expectations moved too, with December hike odds falling to 70.1% from 82.7%.

Crypto did not sit this one out. Bitcoin fell 5.4% to $75,871, below where it traded before the election. Ethereum dropped 7.6% to $2,239, a steeper fall than the Nasdaq. XRP lost 9.8%, and Hyperliquid fell 4.9%. The CMC20 index fell 6.29%. Almost $1.1B in crypto longs were liquidated on the day, before a slight relief rally overnight.

Friday’s October jobs report is next. After a week where the market read the same political outcome two opposite ways inside two sessions, it is fair to wonder how much anyone actually knows right now. We will find out soon…

#5059. Solana’s Shanghai event halted over unauthorized promotion: Report

Solana’s promotional campaigns through domestic entities allegedly violated China’s crypto-related restrictions, an unidentified PBOC official told China’s Financial News.

Chinese authorities halted the Solana Accelerate event in Shanghai over alleged unauthorized promotional activities involving domestic entities, China’s state-backed Financial News reported on Friday.

The outlet cited an unidentified official from a department of the People’s Bank of China (PBOC), who said Solana had promoted its brand through domestic entities, including by placing advertisements on a building in Shanghai’s Lujiazui financial district.

The official also claimed that Solana provides technical services supporting the issuance and trading of cryptocurrencies, which he described as illegal financial activities under Chinese regulations.

It marked the second reported disruption of a Solana event in mainland China in the past two years, highlighting how the country’s restrictions extend beyond digital asset trading.  An October 2025 Solana event in Shenzhen was also cut short over venue capacity and public-safety concerns, according to an X announcement by Solana’s Chinese-speaking community.

In an Oct. 16 X post, Solana Foundation president Lily Liu said organizers postponed the Shanghai event to “comply with local requirements” after an event registration unexpectedly quadrupled, exceeding the event cap pre-registered with local authorities.

In a February notice, the PBOC and several other agencies doubled down on crypto-related restrictions and included a new ban on the unauthorized issuance of yuan-pegged stablecoins.

#5057. Volkswagen gets L3 autonomous driving test permit in China, eyes 2027

Volkswagen Group China now has a permit to test Level 3 autonomous driving on public roads in Hefei, and it says testing is already underway. The permit also covers large-scale driving data collection in the city.

It’s the latest step toward VW’s goal of L3 technology readiness in China by 2027. And the timing is good as the group holds its first China Technology Day in Hefei on Wednesday.

VW says the permit was issued under China’s strictest L3 framework and that its setup meets the country’s latest mandatory safety design standards. It didn’t say which authority issued it, how many vehicles are in the fleet, or which roads they can use.

Hefei makes sense as a base. It’s home to the Volkswagen China Technology Company (VCTC), the group’s largest R&D center outside Germany, and to its Volkswagen Anhui production site.

The software comes from CARIZON, VW’s intelligent driving unit in China, which started as a joint venture between CARIAD and Horizon Robotics. Its driver assistance system is called Hyper Sense 8 (HS8), which VW describes as its first “full-scenario” ADAS.

Behind it is GAIA 2.0, a world model VW built itself. VW says it runs simulation equal to 5 million km of safety testing per day, using about 30 million real-road scenario clips, and it wants to get that to 10 million km per day. VW is also developing its own system-on-chip, the C7H, which it says can support up to Level 4.

Ralf Brandstätter, chairman and CEO of Volkswagen Group China, kept the message on safety. He said the group “will never advance technology at the cost of safety” and called the permit an important step on the road to L3 readiness (our translation from Chinese).

This is a road test permit. VW can’t sell L3 cars to customers with it.

China’s MIIT granted the country’s first L3 product approvals in December 2025 to two sedans: a Changan model capped at 50 km/h on designated Chongqing expressways, and a BAIC Arcfox model capped at 80 km/h on three Beijing routes. GAC’s Hyptec and XPeng already hold L3 road test permits too. So VW isn’t leading here. It’s catching up to Chinese brands on their home turf.

In July, VW, CARIZON, and Horizon Robotics said they would deepen their partnership, giving CARIZON “white-box” access to Horizon’s AI foundation model, and targeted the start of L3 deliveries in the second half of 2027. VW’s L2++ system is rolling out on seven new NEV models from its Chinese joint ventures this year.

VW also leans on XPeng in China. It bought a roughly 5% stake in XPeng in 2023 and co-developed its China zonal electrical architecture, CEA, with the startup.

Outside China, the only Western automaker with a certified L3 system on sale is Mercedes-Benz, whose Drive Pilot works on approved highways in Germany and in California and Nevada. Mercedes also got approval to test Level 4 driving in Beijing back in 2024. Tesla’s FSD remains a Level 2 system everywhere it’s sold, China included.

L3 is where liability moves. When the system is engaged, the automaker is responsible for the driving, not the person in the seat. That’s why so few companies have shipped it, and why the ones that have keep it on a short leash: specific roads, low speeds, mostly traffic jams.

China has been serious about building a framework for automakers to bring this techonology to market, and through it, we are seeing who is interested in this version of autonomy.

Not everyone is. Many automakers, including Tesla, have been talking about “skipping” level 3 and going straight to level 4.

#5056. Tesla to Wind Down Cybertruck Production as Elon Musk Pivots to Robotaxis and AI

Tesla to Wind Down Cybertruck Production as Elon Musk Pivots to Robotaxis and AI

Tesla will stop making the Cybertruck at its Texas Gigafactory around the middle of next year, CEO Elon Musk said on Friday, as the company shifts its focus to autonomous vehicles.

"Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production," Musk tweeted.

Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production

Responding to a user who called the Cybertruck the best vehicle Tesla has ever made, Musk replied: "True, but the Cybertruck fleet will be around for a long time." "Cybertruck was an incredible technology development platform and lives on through Cybercab," Wes Morrill, the truck's lead engineer, wrote in response to Musk's post, thanking the truck's owners and "everyone who contributed to making the future looking like the future."

The stainless-steel pickup, first delivered to customers in late 2023, has struggled to win buyers. Tesla delivered 8,295 vehicles in its "other models" category, which includes the Cybertruck, in the third quarter, down 48% from a year earlier, Electrek reported. That puts the truck's sales at roughly a tenth of the 250,000 units a year that Tesla built its production line to make, according to the outlet.

Musk has made the Cybercab, Tesla's robotaxi, central to his push to turn the carmaker into an AI and robotics company. In April 2025, he said Tesla would launch its first robotaxi service in Austin using Model Y vehicles running its Full Self-Driving software, and begin building Cybercabs in Texas the following year.

Tesla began offering rides in the Cybercab, a two-seater with no steering wheel or pedals that was first unveiled in October 2024, in limited areas of Austin on September 3, Reuters reported. The National Highway Traffic Safety Administration said at the time that it was "evaluating the situation," as federal rules typically require cars to have human controls. Tesla began producing the Cybercab in April, and had 45 of them registered in Texas as of the launch, according to the report.

At the 2024 unveiling, Musk said he expects the Cybercab to cost around $30,000, Auto Express reported.

The shift comes as Musk weighs combining Tesla with SpaceX, which absorbed his AI company xAI earlier this year. Last month, Musk said both companies' boards had formed special committees of independent directors to evaluate a merger, which would reshape his AI empire by bringing rockets, satellites, AI models, robots and cars under one roof. A SpaceX filing has since confirmed that any combination would need sign-off from its "controlling shareholder," Musk.

A merger would also create a Bitcoin treasury behemoth, combining SpaceX's 18,712 BTC and Tesla's 11,509 BTC for a total of 30,221 BTC, worth roughly $2.3 billion at current prices.

Both companies have also blocked their shares from being offered on the tokenized stock venue being built by crypto exchange OKX and NYSE owner ICE.

#5055. Ethereum devs won’t fork Glamsterdam on Hoodi until smart contract risks resolved

Ethereum developers are investigating concerns tied to eight smart contract invariants tied to Glamsterdam’s EIP-8037 and EIP-8038.

Ethereum core developers delayed the Glamsterdam upgrade’s implementation on the Hoodi testnet until security concerns tied to eight smart contract invariants are resolved.

Some existing Ethereum projects rely on assumptions that certain operations would cost a specific amount of gas, which would be invalidated by Glamsterdam’s EIP-8037 and EIP-8038 because they change how gas is charged for certain operations, the Ethereum Foundation said in a Friday blog post.

The decision followed an Oct. 13 report from smart contract auditor ChainSecurity, which found that the two Ethereum Improvement Proposals (EIPs) could break smart contracts that rely on fixed gas-related assumptions when external calls fail.

Delaying the Hoodi testnet fork gives developers more time to identify potential smart contract issues and determine whether changes are needed before Glamsterdam progresses toward mainnet deployment. The Hoodi fork will not proceed until the issues are resolved, added the Ethereum Foundation’s post.

Ethereum’s Glamsterdam upgrade went live on the Sepolia testnet on Oct. 6, in one of its final major rehearsals. The upgrade aims to advance Ethereum’s layer-1 scaling roadmap with enshrined proposer-builder separation, block-level access lists and changes to gas pricing that better reflect the cost of execution, the Ethereum Foundation announced in a Sept. 28 blog post.

#5053. BYD opens pre-orders for its first luxury electric SUV with over 1,000 km CLTC range

BYD opened pre-orders for the new Denza N8, a luxury five-seat electric SUV with a CLTC range of over 1,000 km (621 miles).

“The king of large 5-seater pure electric vehicles,” as BYD’s luxury sub-brand Denza calls it, is now available to pre-order in China.

BYD opened pre-orders for the new Denza N8 on Monday, and the results so far “have been excellent,” according to Li Hui, Denza’s general manager.

The new Denza N8 is “definitely the most anticipated luxury SUV of the year,” Li said. Denza confirmed in August that the new version will use its Blade Battery 2.0 and Flash Charging Technology to deliver “a world-leading 1,003 km pure electric range.”

Although BYD has released several luxury EVs that offer more than 1,000 km of CLTC range, such as the Denza Z9 GT, this is its first electric SUV to break the 1,000 km mark under CLTC conditions.

For context, the plug-in hybrid version the new Denza N8 is replacing offered a combined CLTC range of 1,003 km (623 miles) on a full tank of gas and a full charge.

BYD didn’t reveal pre-order prices, but Li confirmed last week that the Denza N8’s official launch is scheduled for November.

It will go on sale after the new Denza N8L, a slightly longer six-seat version. The N8L is available in two versions: Premium and Flagship. The N8L EV Premium starts at 299,800 RMB, or about $45,000, while the Flagship model is priced from 329,800 RMB ($49,000).

Those who place a reservation with a 2,000 RMB ($200) deposit will receive a 5,000 RMB credit as a limited-time special offer.

Measuring 5,150 mm long, 1,999 mm wide, and 1,820 mm high, with a 3,075 mm wheelbase, the Denza N8 EV is slightly longer than the Kia EV9 and Hyundai IONIQ 9.

Inside, the five-seat electric SUV features a massive 30″ infotainment screen that spans nearly the entire passenger-side dashboard, plus a new 43″ (1.1 meter) panoramic head-up display (PHUD) at the bottom of the windshield.

Other premium features include zero gravity seating in all rows, a three-spoke steering wheel, wireless phone chargers, and ambient lighting.

The luxury SUV is equipped with a roof-mounted LiDAR to support BYD’s God’s Eye 5.0 smart driving assistance system. It also features DiSus-A, BYD’s intelligent air suspension system, and rear-wheel drive.

The new Denza N8 EV will be available with a single rear-wheel-drive (RWD) or a tri-motor all-wheel-drive (AWD) powertrain. The RWD version uses a single motor producing either 320 kW (429 hp) or 370 kW (496 hp).

The tri-motor Denza N8 uses BYD’s e3 platform. The setup includes a 270 kW (362 hp) front motor and two 310 kW (416 hp) rear motors, delivering a combined output of 890 kW (1,193 hp).

Regulatory filings earlier this year revealed it will be available with a 130.15 kWh or 105.79 kWh BYD Blade 2.0 battery, offering CLTC ranges of up to 1,003 km and 800 km, respectively.

BYD will announce prices next month, but given that the Denza N8L starts at 299,800 RMB ($45,000), the standard version will likely be priced below it.

#5052. Here’s what happened in crypto today

Need to know what happened in crypto today? Here is the latest news on daily trends and events impacting Bitcoin price, blockchain, DeFi, Web3 and crypto regulation.

Today in crypto, Binance’s $6.3 billion security incident has left unanswered questions about how the exchange managed its hot wallets and limited the amount attackers could access, exchanges moved to reassure customers about their cold storage reserves following Binance’s hack, and Bitcoin plunged below $76,000 as US stocks erased gains made after the midterm elections.

Binance’s $6.3 billion security incident has left unanswered questions about how the exchange managed its hot wallets and limited the amount attackers could access.

Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said on Oct. 30 that the attack affected the exchange’s own hot wallet. Most customer assets were held in offline cold storage and moved to hot wallets only when users requested withdrawals.

Wallet balances offer only a partial picture of an exchange’s exposure to an attack. Public blockchain records can show funds moving but cannot establish the internal controls governing those transfers, CertiK analyst Jonathan Riss told Cointelegraph.

In its initial report Tuesday, Binance said attackers accessed an internal wallet-signing service to move funds outside its normal withdrawal process. Binance said some technical details would remain confidential to prevent further attacks.

Crypto exchanges were quick to reassure users following the attack on Binance’s hot wallet infrastructure last week, with several companies disclosing how they divide assets between cold storage and internet-connected hot wallets this week.

Crypto exchange Gate on Thursday launched a “Proof of Security” (PoSEC) framework, reporting that 88.26% of its total assets were in cold storage and 11.74% in hot wallets. It claims its cold storage could cover 104.58% of customer assets even if its hot wallets were completely drained.

“By publicly disclosing the distribution of assets between platform cold storage and hot wallets, PoSEC provides visibility into asset storage structures and their ability to cover potential losses under extreme scenarios,” Gate said. It noted the data is still undergoing independent review by a third-party audit firm.

Threat actors attacked Binance’s hot-wallet infrastructure last week, stealing approximately $6.36 billion, most of it in about 20 minutes, according to TRM Labs. Binance CEO Richard Teng said the “vast majority” of user assets are held in cold wallets, but the company has not provided a precise split.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell below $76,000 on Thursday as risk assets abruptly reversed their US midterm elections rally. After making swift gains on the back of a Democrat sweep of the US House of Representatives and Senate, equities saw sharp selling on Thursday as traders rotated out of tech.

Crypto markets liquidated nearly $500 million in positioning in the first hour of the US trading session, per data from CoinGlass, with 24-hour total liquidations nearing $850 million.

Now targeting $76,000, BTC/USD approached the bottom of its one-month trading range. The pair risked losing support at the 50-week simple moving average (SMA), currently at $75,733.

In his latest analysis, crypto trader Rekt Capital noted that Bitcoin was repeating behavior from May, when a period of rangebound moves around $80,000 subsequently gave way to a breakdown and new macro lows of $57,000 in late June.
